"""Integration tests — single combined LLM call, mocked at module level."""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
from unittest.mock import patch

import pytest
from src.data.models import HearsayExample, Decision

_COMBINED = json.dumps({
    "case_id": "t1",
    "arguments": [
        {
            "id": "a1", "text": "Out-of-court statement.",
            "stance_to_claim": "support", "legal_rule": "FRE 801(c)(1)",
            "evidence_span": None, "confidence": 0.9,
            "rubric": {"legal_relevance": 0.9, "factual_grounding": 0.8,
                       "specificity": 0.7, "logical_coherence": 0.9,
                       "fre_801c_applicability": 0.9},
            "tau": 0.85, "justification": "Strong argument.",
        },
        {
            "id": "a2", "text": "Offered for truth of matter asserted.",
            "stance_to_claim": "support", "legal_rule": "FRE 801(c)(2)",
            "evidence_span": None, "confidence": 0.85,
            "rubric": {"legal_relevance": 0.85, "factual_grounding": 0.75,
                       "specificity": 0.7, "logical_coherence": 0.85,
                       "fre_801c_applicability": 0.85},
            "tau": 0.80, "justification": "Good argument.",
        },
    ],
    "relations": [
        {"source": "a1", "target": "phi", "type": "support", "confidence": 0.9},
        {"source": "a2", "target": "phi", "type": "support", "confidence": 0.85},
    ],
})

_CFG = {
    "backend": "gemini",
    "gemini":  {"model": "gemini-2.5-flash", "max_tokens": 4096, "temperature": 0.0},
    "mining":  {"max_arguments": 6, "min_confidence": 0.3,
                "include_neutral_relations": False, "max_retries": 1},
    "qbaf":    {"solver": "df_quad", "max_iterations": 50,
                "convergence_eps": 1e-6, "decision_threshold": 0.5,
                "uncertainty_band": [0.45, 0.55], "phi_initial_strength": 0.5},
    "system":  {"artifact_dir": "/tmp/adss_test"},
}

# Patch at the module-level reference used by each module
_EXT  = "src.mining.extractor._llm.call_llm"
_ORCH = "src.pipeline.orchestrator._llm.call_llm"


def test_full_pipeline_single_call():
    """ONE mock call → valid prediction with strengths."""
    call_count = 0
    def _mock(*a, **kw):
        nonlocal call_count
        call_count += 1
        return _COMBINED

    from src.pipeline.orchestrator import ADSSPipeline
    with patch(_EXT, side_effect=_mock):
        pred = ADSSPipeline(_CFG).run_case(
            HearsayExample(case_id="t1",
                           text="Witness said victim told him 'car was red'.",
                           split="test"),
            save_artifacts=False,
        )

    assert call_count == 1, f"Expected 1 LLM call, got {call_count}"
    assert pred.case_id == "t1"
    assert pred.decision in Decision
    assert 0.0 <= pred.sigma_phi <= 1.0
    assert len(pred.extraction.arguments) == 2
    assert len(pred.strengths) == 2
    assert pred.strengths[0].tau > 0.1  # tau computed from rubric weights


def test_hitl_edit_tau():
    from src.pipeline.orchestrator import ADSSPipeline
    from src.data.models import HITLIntervention

    with patch(_EXT, return_value=_COMBINED):
        pipeline = ADSSPipeline(_CFG)
        pred = pipeline.run_case(
            HearsayExample(case_id="t1", text="Witness test.", split="test"),
            save_artifacts=False,
        )

    assert "a1" in pred.solver_output.graph.nodes
    old_sigma = pred.sigma_phi

    pred2 = pipeline.apply_hitl_intervention(pred, HITLIntervention(
        intervention_type="edit_tau", target_id="a1",
        old_value=0.85, new_value=0.1,
    ))
    assert pred2.sigma_phi != old_sigma
    assert len(pred2.hitl_interventions) == 1


def test_baseline_cot():
    from src.pipeline.orchestrator import BaselineRunner
    cot_response = "The statement is out-of-court and offered for truth.\nAnswer: Yes"

    with patch(_ORCH, return_value=cot_response):
        result = BaselineRunner(_CFG).run_cot(
            HearsayExample(case_id="b1", text="test", split="test", label="Yes")
        )
    assert result.decision == Decision.YES


def test_lmstudio_backend_routes_correctly():
    cfg_ls = dict(_CFG, backend="lmstudio",
                  lmstudio={"model": "qwen/qwen3-30b-a3b",
                             "base_url": "http://127.0.0.1:1234/v1",
                             "max_tokens": 4096, "temperature": 0.0})
    import src.utils.lmstudio_client as _ls
    with patch.object(_ls, "call_lmstudio", return_value=_COMBINED) as mock_ls:
        from src.pipeline.orchestrator import ADSSPipeline
        pred = ADSSPipeline(cfg_ls).run_case(
            HearsayExample(case_id="t2", text="test", split="test"),
            save_artifacts=False,
        )
        assert mock_ls.called
    assert len(pred.extraction.arguments) == 2


===== FILE: C:\Users\nkaran\Dropbox\work account\Diplomatikes\diplo oiko2k\app\adss_hearsay\tests\test_solver.py =====
"""Unit tests for QBAF solvers (no LLM calls)."""
import pytest
from src.data.models import QBAFGraph, QBAFEdge, QBAFNode, RelationType
from src.qbaf.solver import DFQuADSolver, QESemanticsSolver

_CFG = {"qbaf": {"max_iterations": 100, "convergence_eps": 1e-8,
                  "decision_threshold": 0.5, "uncertainty_band": [0.45, 0.55]}}


def _simple():
    return QBAFGraph(
        nodes={
            "a1":  QBAFNode(id="a1",  text="s", tau=0.8, sigma=0.8),
            "a2":  QBAFNode(id="a2",  text="a", tau=0.3, sigma=0.3),
            "phi": QBAFNode(id="phi", text="c", tau=0.5, sigma=0.5, is_claim=True),
        },
        edges=[
            QBAFEdge(source="a1", target="phi", type=RelationType.SUPPORT, confidence=1.0),
            QBAFEdge(source="a2", target="phi", type=RelationType.ATTACK,  confidence=1.0),
        ],
    )


def _solo():
    return QBAFGraph(
        nodes={"phi": QBAFNode(id="phi", text="c", tau=0.5, sigma=0.5, is_claim=True)},
        edges=[],
    )


class TestDFQuAD:
    def test_no_edges_sigma_equals_tau(self):
        assert abs(DFQuADSolver(_CFG).solve(_solo()).sigma_phi - 0.5) < 1e-6

    def test_support_increases_sigma(self):
        g = QBAFGraph(
            nodes={"a1": QBAFNode(id="a1", text="s", tau=0.9, sigma=0.9),
                   "phi": QBAFNode(id="phi", text="c", tau=0.5, sigma=0.5, is_claim=True)},
            edges=[QBAFEdge(source="a1", target="phi", type=RelationType.SUPPORT, confidence=1.0)],
        )
        assert DFQuADSolver(_CFG).solve(g).sigma_phi > 0.5

    def test_attack_decreases_sigma(self):
        g = QBAFGraph(
            nodes={"a1": QBAFNode(id="a1", text="a", tau=0.9, sigma=0.9),
                   "phi": QBAFNode(id="phi", text="c", tau=0.5, sigma=0.5, is_claim=True)},
            edges=[QBAFEdge(source="a1", target="phi", type=RelationType.ATTACK, confidence=1.0)],
        )
        assert DFQuADSolver(_CFG).solve(g).sigma_phi < 0.5

    def test_sigma_in_unit_interval(self):
        out = DFQuADSolver(_CFG).solve(_simple())
        assert all(0.0 <= v <= 1.0 for v in out.sigma_all.values())

    def test_converged(self):
        assert DFQuADSolver(_CFG).solve(_simple()).converged


class TestQESemantics:
    def test_no_edges_sigma_equals_tau(self):
        assert abs(QESemanticsSolver(_CFG).solve(_solo()).sigma_phi - 0.5) < 1e-6

    def test_support_increases_sigma(self):
        g = QBAFGraph(
            nodes={"a1": QBAFNode(id="a1", text="s", tau=0.9, sigma=0.9),
                   "phi": QBAFNode(id="phi", text="c", tau=0.5, sigma=0.5, is_claim=True)},
            edges=[QBAFEdge(source="a1", target="phi", type=RelationType.SUPPORT, confidence=1.0)],
        )
        assert QESemanticsSolver(_CFG).solve(g).sigma_phi > 0.5

    def test_sigma_in_unit_interval(self):
        out = QESemanticsSolver(_CFG).solve(_simple())
        assert all(0.0 <= v <= 1.0 for v in out.sigma_all.values())

```bash

```

```bash

python scripts/run_eval.py --split test
```

## Configuration

All thresholds, model names, and solver semantics live in `configs/config.yaml`.
Key settings:

| Key | Default | Description |
|-----|---------|-------------|
| `qbaf.solver` | `df_quad` | `df_quad` or `qe_semantics` |
| `qbaf.decision_threshold` | `0.5` | σ(φ) cut-off for Yes/No |
| `qbaf.uncertainty_band` | `[0.45, 0.55]` | UAE escalation zone |
| `mining.max_arguments` | `8` | Max arguments extracted per case |
| `anthropic.model` | `claude-sonnet-4-20250514` | LLM for all modules |

===== FILE: C:\Users\nkaran\Dropbox\work account\Diplomatikes\diplo oiko2k\app\adss_hearsay\README.md =====
# ADSS-Hearsay
**Neuro-Symbolic Argumentative Decision Support System for LegalBench Hearsay (FRE 801(c))**
Powered by **Google Gemini**.

---

## Quick start

```bash
# 1. Clone & create venv
python -m venv .venv && source .venv/bin/activate

# 2. Install
pip install -r requirements.txt

# 3. Set your Gemini API key (free at https://aistudio.google.com/app/apikey)
export GEMINI_API_KEY=your_key_here

# 4. Launch the HITL dashboard
streamlit run src/dashboard/app.py

# 5. Run evaluation (test split, 20 samples)
python scripts/run_eval.py --split test --max 20

# 6. Run case studies
python scripts/case_studies.py

# 7. Run unit tests (no API key needed)
pytest tests/ -v
```

---

## Architecture

```
Input narrative
      │
      ▼
┌──────────────────────┐
│ Module A: Extraction │  Gemini → JSON arguments + relations
└──────────┬───────────┘
           │
           ▼
┌──────────────────────┐
│ Module B: Strength   │  Gemini → τ(αᵢ) ∈ [0.1, 1.0]
└──────────┬───────────┘
           │
           ▼
┌──────────────────────┐
│ Module C: QBAF       │  DF-QuAD or QE → σ(φ) ∈ [0,1]
└──────────┬───────────┘
           │
    ┌──────┴──────┐
    ▼             ▼
 Yes / No      UNCERTAIN → escalation log
           │
           ▼
┌──────────────────────┐
│ Module D: HITL       │  Streamlit dashboard
└──────────────────────┘
```

---

## Solvers

| Solver | Equation |
|--------|----------|
| **DF-QuAD** (default) | `σ(x) = τ(x) + (1−τ)·CS(x) − τ·CA(x)` where `CS = 1−∏(1−σ(s))` |
| **QE Semantics** | `σ(x) = τ(x)·(1+Σσ(S)) / (1+Σσ(S)+Σσ(A))` |

Switch via `configs/config.yaml` → `qbaf.solver: qe_semantics`

---

## Configuration (`configs/config.yaml`)

| Key | Default | Description |
|-----|---------|-------------|
| `gemini.model` | `gemini-2.0-flash` | Gemini model for all LLM calls |
| `qbaf.solver` | `df_quad` | `df_quad` or `qe_semantics` |
| `qbaf.decision_threshold` | `0.5` | σ(φ) cut-off |
| `qbaf.uncertainty_band` | `[0.45, 0.55]` | UAE escalation zone |
| `mining.max_arguments` | `8` | Max args per case |

---

## Output artefacts

```
artifacts/
├── <case_id>_prediction.json
├── case_studies/
│   ├── cs_001.json  (correct initial)
│   ├── cs_002.json  (HITL correction)
│   └── cs_003.json  (uncertainty escalation)
└── evaluation/
    ├── full_adss_report.json
    ├── full_adss_predictions.csv
    └── summary.csv
```

================ FILE: prompts/combined.txt ================

You are an expert AI reasoning assistant for decision support and argumentation analysis.

DECISION PROBLEM (central claim phi):
{decision_problem}

TASK: Analyse the narrative and extract up to {max_arguments} arguments relevant to
whether the decision problem is true or false.

STANCE RULES — assign carefully:
- "support" : this argument provides evidence that the claim IS true
- "attack"  : this argument provides evidence that the claim is NOT true / an exception applies
- "neutral" : the argument is genuinely irrelevant or meta-commentary about the decision itself

Extract arguments that take a clear position. Avoid neutral unless the argument truly
cannot be classified as support or attack.

OUTPUT RULES:
- ONE JSON object only. No markdown, no explanation, no preamble.
- Do NOT copy narrative text verbatim into JSON fields — paraphrase concisely.
- "text" and "legal_rule" fields: max 80 characters each.
- Include rubric scores and tau for each argument.
- Relations only between argument ids (never "phi" as source).
- Use domain-appropriate rules (legal, medical, ethical, factual, etc.) in "legal_rule".

{
  "case_id": "{case_id}",
  "arguments": [
    {
      "id": "a1",
      "text": "concise argument under 80 chars",
      "stance_to_claim": "support",
      "legal_rule": "relevant rule or principle",
      "evidence_span": null,
      "confidence": 0.9,
      "rubric": {
        "legal_relevance": 0.9,
        "factual_grounding": 0.8,
        "specificity": 0.7,
        "logical_coherence": 0.9,
        "fre_801c_applicability": 0.9
      },
      "tau": 0.85,
      "justification": "one sentence explaining stance"
    }
  ],
  "relations": [
    {"source": "a1", "target": "a2", "type": "support", "confidence": 0.8}
  ]
}

Narrative (Case {case_id}):
{narrative}



================ FILE: prompts/cot_baseline.txt ================

You are an expert in evidence law. Decide whether the statement described in the following legal narrative is HEARSAY under FRE 801(c).

FRE 801(c) defines hearsay as an out-of-court statement offered to prove the truth of the matter asserted.

Think step-by-step:
1. Identify the statement(s) at issue.
2. Determine whether each statement was made out of court.
3. Determine the purpose for which the statement is offered.
4. Apply the hearsay definition and any applicable exception or exclusion.
5. State your conclusion.

At the very end, output EXACTLY one of these two lines:
  Answer: Yes
  Answer: No

## Narrative
{narrative}



================ FILE: prompts/extraction.txt ================

You are an expert legal AI assistant specialising in evidence law and the Federal Rules of Evidence (FRE).

## Task
Analyse the legal narrative (provided separately below) and extract structured arguments relevant to the central claim:

  phi = "The statement is hearsay under FRE 801(c)."

Under FRE 801(c), a statement is hearsay if:
  1. It is an out-of-court statement.
  2. It is offered to prove the truth of the matter asserted.

Factors that ATTACK (undermine) the hearsay claim:
  - Party-opponent admissions (FRE 801(d)(2))
  - Prior inconsistent/consistent statements (FRE 801(d)(1))
  - Excited utterances (FRE 803(2))
  - Present sense impressions (FRE 803(1))
  - Statements for medical diagnosis (FRE 803(4))
  - Business records (FRE 803(6))
  - Not offered for truth (verbal act, legally operative words, effect on listener)

## Output instructions
- Extract up to {max_arguments} arguments.
- Each argument: "support" = supports hearsay; "attack" = undermines hearsay.
- For each pair of arguments, classify their relation as "support", "attack", or "neutral".
- Do NOT include the narrative text anywhere in the JSON.
- Return ONLY the JSON object below — no markdown fences, no explanation, no preamble.
- All string values must use standard ASCII double quotes. Do not use smart quotes.
- Keep argument "text" and "legal_rule" fields short (under 120 characters each).

## JSON schema (return exactly this structure)
{
  "case_id": "CASE_ID_PLACEHOLDER",
  "arguments": [
    {
      "id": "a1",
      "text": "short argument text here",
      "stance_to_claim": "support",
      "legal_rule": "FRE 801(c)(1)",
      "evidence_span": null,
      "confidence": 0.9
    }
  ],
  "relations": [
    {
      "source": "a1",
      "target": "a2",
      "type": "support",
      "confidence": 0.8
    }
  ]
}

## Narrative (Case ID: {case_id})
{narrative}

================ FILE: prompts/generic_combined.txt ================

You are an expert legal argument-mining and decision-support assistant.

Your task is to extract a structured argumentative representation from the case narrative for a Quantitative Bipolar Argumentation Framework (QBAF).

You MUST return exactly one valid JSON object and nothing else.
Do not wrap the JSON in markdown fences.
Do not include explanatory prose outside the JSON.

CASE ID:
{case_id}

DECISION PROBLEM / TARGET CLAIM φ:
{decision_problem}

IMPORTANT RULE ABOUT φ:
- If the decision problem above is concrete and non-empty, use it exactly as the field "phi".
- Do NOT replace it with another inferred question.
- Only infer a new phi if the decision problem is blank or says exactly: "Infer the main yes/no decision from the case."

CASE NARRATIVE:
{narrative}

MAX ARGUMENTS:
{max_arguments}

GENERAL OBJECTIVE
Extract all distinct argumentative components that are genuinely relevant to deciding φ.
The goal is not to summarize the case as one argument. The goal is to decompose the reasoning into multiple legally or decision-relevant components whenever the case supports such decomposition.

For LegalBench hearsay / FRE 801(c) cases, decompose the reasoning into components such as:
1. whether the statement was made out of court;
2. whether the statement contains an assertion;
3. whether the statement is offered to prove the truth of the matter asserted;
4. whether the statement is offered for another purpose, such as notice, effect on listener, impeachment, verbal act, or state of mind;
5. whether an exclusion such as party-opponent admission under FRE 801(d)(2) appears relevant;
6. whether an exception-like consideration is present, while keeping the distinction between definitional hearsay and admissibility exceptions clear;
7. whether the evidence is merely circumstantial/non-assertive rather than an out-of-court statement;
8. whether the narrative contains facts that are irrelevant to the hearsay target claim.

For LegalBench contract NLI / confidentiality-of-agreement cases, decompose the reasoning into components such as:
1. whether the contract context contains an explicit confidentiality clause;
2. whether the clause applies to the relevant party or information type;
3. whether the target confidentiality statement is directly entailed by the contract text;
4. whether the target statement is contradicted by the contract text;
5. whether the target statement is merely not mentioned or not inferable from the contract;
6. whether exceptions, carve-outs, duration limits, scope limitations, or permitted disclosures affect the confidentiality obligation;
7. whether definitions such as Confidential Information, Recipient, Disclosing Party, or Agreement scope support or weaken the target statement.

For generic non-legal decision problems, decompose the reasoning into components such as:
1. facts supporting the target claim;
2. facts attacking the target claim;
3. causal links;
4. rule/principle applicability;
5. exceptions, defenses, counterarguments, or alternative explanations;
6. evidence of harm, benefit, liability, feasibility, risk, preference, or uncertainty.

DO NOT INVENT FACTS
- Use only facts contained in the narrative.
- You may make legally or logically necessary inferences from the narrative, but do not introduce new evidence.
- If the case truly contains only one relevant component, return only one argument.
- However, if multiple legal reasoning components are implicit in the text, separate them into distinct arguments.

ARGUMENT REQUIREMENTS
Each argument must be a distinct reasoning component.
Avoid duplicate arguments.
Avoid generic boilerplate unless it is tied to the case facts.
Neutral or irrelevant components may be included with stance_to_claim = "neutral", but they will be excluded from the QBAF graph.

ARGUMENT GRANULARITY RULES
- If the narrative contains an explicit list of arguments, reasons, objections, counterarguments, risk factors, rebuttals, or considerations, preserve them as separate argumentative components whenever possible.
- Do NOT merge distinct listed arguments into broad meta-arguments.
- For structured or semi-structured inputs, use as many arguments as needed up to {max_arguments}.
- Only prefer 3-6 arguments for short unstructured narratives that do not contain a detailed argument list.
- If the narrative contains labels such as A1, A2, B1, C1, preserve the labeled item as a distinct argument node whenever possible.

STANCE DEFINITIONS
- "support": the argument increases the acceptability of φ.
- "attack": the argument decreases the acceptability of φ.
- "neutral": the component is background or irrelevant to φ.

For hearsay cases:
- Out-of-court + assertion + offered for truth usually SUPPORTS φ.
- Offered for notice/effect on listener/verbal act/impeachment usually ATTACKS φ.
- Party-opponent exclusion under FRE 801(d)(2) usually ATTACKS φ because the statement is treated as non-hearsay.
- Pure circumstantial evidence that does not depend on an out-of-court assertion usually ATTACKS φ.
- Irrelevant facts should be marked NEUTRAL.

For contract NLI cases:
- A clause or definition that directly entails the target confidentiality statement SUPPORTS φ.
- A clause that contradicts, narrows, excludes, or limits the target statement ATTACKS φ.
- Absence of relevant contractual support should usually ATTACK φ if the task is entailment.
- Unrelated contractual provisions should be marked NEUTRAL.

RELATION REQUIREMENTS
Create both:
1. argument-to-claim relations, where arguments support or attack phi;
2. argument-to-argument relations, where one argument supports, attacks, rebuts, undercuts, qualifies, or weakens another argument.

- Every non-neutral argument should normally have a direct relation to "phi", except when its primary role is clearly to support, attack, rebut, undercut, qualify, or weaken another argument.
- In that case, an argument-to-argument relation is sufficient.

You MUST add argument-to-argument relations when:
- a counterargument says that another argument is insufficient;
- a counterargument gives an alternative explanation;
- a rebuttal says that a risk or objection is acceptable, outweighed, or not decisive;
- one argument narrows, qualifies, or limits another argument.

Use relation type:
- "support" when the source strengthens the target;
- "attack" when the source weakens, rebuts, undercuts, or challenges the target;
- "neutral" only when there is no argumentative interaction.

You must infer not only relations between arguments and phi, but also relations between arguments.

An argument attacks another argument when it:
- challenges the sufficiency of that argument;
- challenges the relevance of that argument;
- challenges the causal link from that argument to phi;
- provides an alternative explanation for that argument's evidence;
- weakens the evidential value of that argument;
- rebuts a risk, objection, or counterargument;
- says that a consideration does not automatically imply the conclusion.

When an argument weakens the inference from another argument to phi, encode this as an attack relation from the weakening argument to the original argument.

Examples:
- "Severe symptoms alone do not prove surgery is necessary" attacks an argument saying "Symptoms are severe and persistent" when the latter supports surgery.
- "Falls may have other causes besides spinal stenosis" attacks an argument saying "Function is clearly impaired" or "falls support surgery".
- "Neurologic decline needs objective confirmation" attacks an argument claiming progressive neurologic decline if objective confirmation is not established.
- "Surgical risk may be acceptable given severe disability and failed conservative treatment" attacks an argument saying surgical risks count against surgery.
- "Controlled diabetes and age do not automatically rule out surgery" attacks an argument saying age and diabetes count against surgery.
- "A difficult recovery may still be justified if expected benefit is substantial" attacks an argument saying difficult recovery counts against surgery.

Do not collapse these into generic direct attacks on phi if the target argument can be identified.

STRENGTH / TAU REQUIREMENTS
For each non-neutral argument, assign tau in [0.1, 1.0].
Tau reflects intrinsic strength before QBAF propagation.
Use this rubric:
- relevance: direct relevance to φ;
- factual_grounding: support from the narrative text;
- specificity: case-specific detail;
- logical_coherence: clear inference from fact to claim;
- applicability: fit with the relevant legal/domain principle.

OUTPUT JSON SCHEMA
Return exactly this structure:
{
  "case_id": "{case_id}",
  "phi": "{decision_problem}",
  "arguments": [
    {
      "id": "a1",
      "text": "Concise argument text grounded in the narrative.",
      "stance_to_claim": "support|attack|neutral",
      "legal_rule": "Relevant legal/domain rule or reasoning principle.",
      "evidence_span": "Exact or near-exact text span from the narrative, or null if not available.",
      "confidence": 0.0,
      "tau": 0.0,
      "rubric": {
        "relevance": 0.0,
        "factual_grounding": 0.0,
        "specificity": 0.0,
        "logical_coherence": 0.0,
        "applicability": 0.0
      },
      "justification": "Brief explanation of why this argument has this stance and strength."
    }
  ],
  "relations": [
    {
      "source": "a1",
      "target": "phi",
      "type": "support|attack|neutral",
      "confidence": 0.0
    }
  ]
}

For semi-structured inputs with listed arguments, objections, or rebuttals:
- First preserve the listed items as separate arguments.
- Then infer the most specific argument-to-argument relations among them.
- Do not satisfy the relation requirement only by connecting every argument to phi.
- If a counterargument clearly targets a specific earlier argument, create an argument-to-argument attack relation.
- If a rebuttal clearly responds to a risk, objection, or counterargument, create an argument-to-argument attack relation against that risk/objection/counterargument.

VALIDATION RULES
- The JSON must be parseable by Python json.loads.
- Relation source and target must refer to existing argument ids or "phi".
- Argument ids must be unique.
- If the narrative provides labels such as A1, A2, B1, C1, use those labels as the argument ids.
- Otherwise use a1, a2, a3, ...
- Use "phi" exactly for the target claim node.
- Do not output more than {max_arguments} arguments.
- Do not omit tau for non-neutral arguments.
- Every non-neutral argument should normally have a direct relation to "phi", except when its primary role is clearly to support, attack, rebut, undercut, qualify, or weaken another argument.
- In that case, an argument-to-argument relation is sufficient.





================ FILE: prompts/strength.txt ================

You are a legal argument strength evaluator specialising in FRE 801(c) hearsay law.

## Task
Score the following argument on five dimensions, each on a scale of 0.0 to 1.0:

1. legal_relevance         – How directly does this argument address FRE 801(c)?
2. factual_grounding       – Is the argument grounded in specific facts from the narrative?
3. specificity             – How precise and non-generic is the argument?
4. logical_coherence       – Is the internal reasoning sound and non-contradictory?
5. fre_801c_applicability  – How correctly is the relevant FRE rule applied?

## Scoring guidance
  0.9–1.0 : highly specific, legally precise, strongly grounded, logically flawless
  0.7–0.8 : strong but with minor gaps
  0.5–0.6 : plausible but incomplete or somewhat generic
  0.3–0.4 : generic or weakly grounded
  0.1–0.2 : poor quality or mostly irrelevant

Compute tau as: (legal_relevance*0.30 + factual_grounding*0.20 + specificity*0.15 + logical_coherence*0.15 + fre_801c_applicability*0.20), clamped to [0.1, 1.0].

## Return ONLY this JSON (no preamble, no markdown fences):
{
  "argument_id": "<id>",
  "rubric": {
    "legal_relevance": 0.0,
    "factual_grounding": 0.0,
    "specificity": 0.0,
    "logical_coherence": 0.0,
    "fre_801c_applicability": 0.0
  },
  "tau": 0.0,
  "justification": "<one sentence>"
}

## Narrative
{narrative}

## Argument to score
ID: {arg_id}
Text: {arg_text}
Stance to claim: {stance}
Legal rule: {legal_rule}
